  "content": "# LIBERO-Pro Hybrid (Pi0.5 + LLM-in-the-loop) \u2014 Perception-Isolated Guide\n\nYou are picking up the **LIBERO-Pro** evaluation track in **perception-isolated**\nmode \u2014 the only mode in this repository (the legacy oracle-state mode is not\nincluded here).\n\n> **Pi0.5 only does the grasp (`pi0_pick`). The LLM (you) handles every motion\n> (`move_to`), every release, sequencing, retries \u2014 and you do not get GT object\n> coordinates. You localize objects yourself from the depth + camera calibration\n> the runtime dumps each step.**\n\nThis document layers on the base playbook. Read it first:\n\n- [`strict_hybrid_guide.md`](./strict_hybrid_guide.md) \u2014 the perception protocol:\n  back-projection localization, the perception artifacts\n  (`agentview_high.png`, `agentview_world_high.npz`, and\n  `agentview_metadata.json`),\n  the primitive vocabulary, the Rules (0/1/2/4/5), and the `strict_perception`\n  audit format. **This is the source of truth for *how you localize*.**\n- **This file** \u2014 LIBERO-Pro\u2013specific setup, the four perturbation axes, the Pi0\n  fullshot baseline, the frame split, and how perception isolation changes the\n  P2-swap story.\n\n> Your task comes from the initial `view_env_state` result's `task_language`;\n> the BDDL is FORBIDDEN.\n> Read the authoritative instruction (the BDDL `:language` tag, coord-free) that\n> the runtime injects and obey it verbatim. Do **not** scrape the BDDL or import\n> the benchmark: that is error-prone (wrong task-map index \u2192 wrong task) and a\n> perception-isolation breach (the `:init` block holds the GT coordinates this\n> mode withholds). For swap fixtures, localize them **visually** (\u00a73.5), never\n> from `:init`. You never hand-roll projection math \u2014 `back_project` applies\n> `K\u207b\u00b9` + the cam\u2192world extrinsic and returns the surface `world_xyz` under a\n> pixel; just use it.\n\nWhenever this guide says \"see the perception protocol\" or \"the localization\nsnippet\", it means `strict_hybrid_guide.md`. **Every rule there applies here**;\nthis guide only *adds* PRO constraints and tooling. Runner contract: call the\nstructured MCP tools (the runner owns the env server \u2014 do not start/stop it, and\nissue no file-based commands); it is a single-episode run \u2014 no `reset`/`exit`,\nrecover in place or write an honest failure audit and `finish`.\n\n## 0. What's different from oracle PRO mode (read this first)\n\n| | oracle mode (not in this repo) | **perception (this guide)** |\n|---|---|---|\n| how launched | oracle-state run | `rpent/cli/main.py --libero-type pro` (or `LIBERO_TYPE=pro`); perception artifacts always dumped, coords withheld |\n| returned `state` objects | full `objects:{name:[x,y,z]}` | **`object_names:[\u2026]` only \u2014 NO coords** |\n| how you learn the task | env prompt / scrape BDDL | **initial `task_language` returned by `view_env_state`** (authoritative `:language`, coord-free) \u2014 never read the BDDL |\n| extra obs artifacts | agentview RGB only | **logical agentview/wrist image, depth, world-map, and metadata keys, including `agentview_high.png` and `wrist_high.png`** \u2014 geometry consumed through `back_project` |\n| cameras | agentview only | **agentview (fixed, ~1m \u2192 \u00b18-13cm) + eye-in-hand wrist (moves with gripper, \u00b11-2cm when <20cm to target)** \u2014 see \u00a73.6 |\n| how P2 swap is solved | read swapped coords from oracle state | **localize the swapped objects by `back_project`** |\n| how a swapped *fixture* site is found | read the swap BDDL `:init` block | **localize the fixture visually** (see \u00a73.5) |\n| run budget | short | **larger** \u2014 perception localization + manipulation is slower (raise `--max-turns` / `--planner-timeout-s`) |\n| audit `regime` | `strict` | `strict_perception` |\n| which image you pick pixels in | agentview RGB | `agentview_high.png` (or `agentview.png` at low resolution) for **pixel-picking**; never use `agentview_policy.png` for back-projection |\n\n**The single most important conceptual shift.** In oracle PRO mode the headline\nis \"the hybrid beats Pi0 on P2 because it reads the *swapped* coordinates straight\nout of oracle state, while Pi0 is prompt-/memory-blind.\" In **perception**\nmode there are no coordinates to read \u2014 so the hybrid's P2 win now comes from\n**seeing where the object is and back-projecting it**. This is a *stronger* claim\n(no oracle state at all), and it is the whole point of running PRO in perception\nmode: P1 (Task) is still won by reading the *language*; P2 (Position) is now won by\n*perception*, not by an oracle.\n\n## 1. Why LIBERO-Pro\n\nLIBERO-Pro\n([paper](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.03827), [repo](https://github.com/Zxy-MLlab/LIBERO-PRO))\nperturbs each base task along five axes; all end-to-end VLAs (OpenVLA / Pi0 /\nPi0.5 / UniVLA) collapse on the two strongest:\n\n| Axis | Suffix | Paper column | What changes | Headline result |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| **Task** | `_task` | **P1** | Instruction + goal predicate inverted | All VLAs \u2248 0.0 |\n| **Position** | `_swap` | **P2** | Object **and fixture** initial positions swapped | All VLAs 0.0\u20130.4 |\n| Semantic | `_lan` | \u2014 | Instruction paraphrased; goal unchanged | VLAs handle (memorize visual) |\n| Object | `_object` | \u2014 | Object appearance / colour / scale | VLA visual policy stressed |\n| Environment | `_environment` | \u2014 | Table / scene swapped | Visual policy stressed |\n\nThe agentic hybrid wins on **P1 and P2** by routing the language channel and the\n*perceived* spatial-state channel through the LLM. Object and Environment\nperturbations enter through the Pi0 vision channel and the hybrid inherits the\nVLA's weakness there \u2014 declare that upfront, don't oversell. **In perception\nmode, the Object/Environment axes also stress your own localization** (a\nrecolored or rescaled object is harder to pixel-pick), so lean on the\nmulti-pixel-median tip from the perception protocol.\n\n## 2. Setup (do these once on a fresh checkout)\n\nRun the idempotent installer from the repo root:\n\n```bash\nbash scripts/install_libero_pro_plus.sh\n```\n\nIt does all four steps below: the liberopro editable install, applies the\nbenchmark-registration patch, syncs the authoritative HF dataset snapshot, and\nverifies with the `get_benchmark(...).get_task(0).language` check. The perception\nobservables (depth + both cameras + hi-res) are unconditional once the runner\nlaunches with `--libero-type pro`; there is nothing perception-specific to\ninstall beyond the PRO setup itself.\n\n### 2.1. LIBERO-PRO repo\n\nCloned at `${LIBERO_PRO_PATH:-/path/to/LIBERO-PRO}/` from\n`https://github.com/RLinf/LIBERO-PRO.git` and installed editable into the openpi\nvenv:\n\n```bash\npython -m pip show liberopro\n# Name: liberopro  Version: 0.1.0  Location: ${LIBERO_PRO_PATH:-/path/to/LIBERO-PRO}\n```\n\n### 2.2. Apply the benchmark-registration patch\n\nThe upstream `__init__.py` does **not** expose the 16 perturbation suites through\n`get_benchmark()`. Our patch\n[`scripts/liberopro_register_perturbations.patch`](../../../../scripts/liberopro_register_perturbations.patch)\nadds them and overrides `Task.language` to read each BDDL's actual `:language`\ntag (so the perturbed instruction reaches Pi0 / hybrid).\n\n```bash\ncd ${LIBERO_PRO_PATH:-/path/to/LIBERO-PRO}\ngit apply <repo-root>/scripts/liberopro_register_perturbations.patch\n```\n\nIf already applied (likely), `git status -s` shows clean. If you reinstall\nliberopro, re-apply.\n\n### 2.3. Huggingface dataset (authoritative)\n\nThe LIBERO-PRO git repo ships **incomplete / broken** init files for several\nperturbation suites (e.g. `libero_spatial_swap` has 0 BDDLs; some\n`libero_spatial_task` `.pruned_init` files are 0 bytes). Treat the git repo as\nunreliable for perturbation data. The full, correct set lives on Huggingface\n([`zhouxueyang/LIBERO-Pro`](https://huggingface.co/datasets/zhouxueyang/LIBERO-Pro)),\npersisted locally at:\n\n```\n${LIBEROPRO_DATASET_PATH:-/path/to/liberopro_hf}/\n\u251c\u2500\u2500 bddl_files/                    16 perturbation suites, 10 BDDLs each\n\u2514\u2500\u2500 init_files/                    16 perturbation suites, 10 init files each\n```\n\nCovers `{libero_spatial, libero_object, libero_goal, libero_10} \u00d7 {swap, task,\nlan, object}`. The installer syncs this into the liberopro install (overwriting\nthe broken upstream files); if the persistent copy is gone, re-download with:\n\n```bash\npython -c \"\nfrom huggingface_hub import snapshot_download\nsnapshot_download(repo_id='zhouxueyang/LIBERO-Pro', repo_type='dataset',\n                  local_dir='${LIBEROPRO_DATASET_PATH:-/path/to/liberopro_hf}',\n                  allow_patterns=['bddl_files/**','init_files/**'])\"\n```\n\n### 2.4. Verify\n\n```bash\nLIBERO_TYPE=pro python -c \"\nimport liberopro.liberopro.benchmark as bench\nfor n in ['libero_spatial_task','libero_spatial_swap','libero_spatial_lan']:\n    b = bench.get_benchmark(n)(); t = b.get_task(0)\n    print(f'{n} t0: {t.language!r}  trials={len(b.get_task_init_states(0))}')\"\n```\n\nExpected:\n```\nlibero_spatial_task t0: 'Pick the akita black bowl not between the plate and the ramekin and place it on the plate'  trials=50\nlibero_spatial_swap t0: 'Pick the akita black bowl between the plate and the ramekin and place it on the plate'  trials=50\nlibero_spatial_lan  t0: 'lift the black bowl between the plate and ramekin and set it on the plate'  trials=50\n```\n\n## 3. PRO-specific environment gotchas\n\nEverything in `strict_hybrid_guide.md` applies. The following are **additional**\nPRO constraints (most live in detail at [`env_calibration.md`](./env_calibration.md)).\n\n### 3.1. Three scene frames, picked per-task\n\nPRO scenes use one of three table fixtures; the eef home z differs by up to\n~0.9 m.\n\n| Fixture | eef home z | Table top z | xy reachable | Where |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| `living_room_table` | \u2248 0.68 | \u2248 0.43 | `(x\u2208\u00b10.30, y\u2208\u00b10.30)` | basket / plate / pudding |\n| `kitchen_table` | \u2248 1.17 | \u2248 0.90 | `(x\u2208\u00b10.30, y\u2208\u00b10.30)` | stove / cabinet / drawer / microwave |\n| `object` (low table) | \u2248 0.26 | \u2248 0.0 | `(x\u2208\u00b10.30, y\u2208\u00b10.30)` | `libero_object` grocery-into-basket |\n\n**Mandatory check at session start: read `state.robot0_eef_pos[2]` from\n`view_env_state({\"step\": 0})`.** \u2248 0.68 \u2192 LIVING_ROOM; \u2248 1.17 \u2192 KITCHEN;\n\u2248 0.26 \u2192 OBJECT. Pick `pre_pos_z` / `carry_z` / `release_z` accordingly (per-item\nOBJECT-frame altitudes are in\n`memory/libero/global/project_libero_object_pro_done.md`). Sending a\nwrong-frame z (e.g. KITCHEN coordinates while the env is in LIVING_ROOM frame)\ncrashes the env worker (EOFError, silent state loss).\n\n> **Perception note.** This proprioceptive z is *not* an object coordinate \u2014 it's\n> the robot's own pose, which the returned `state` still gives you in perception mode.\n> Reading it to pick the frame is fine. It also doubles as a free depth sanity\n> check: your back-projected table z should sit \u22480.25 m below eef home z (\u22480.43 in\n> LIVING_ROOM, \u22480.90 in KITCHEN). If a back-projection lands far from that, you\n> picked a wrong pixel.\n\nFor `libero_spatial_*` you are always in **KITCHEN frame**. Standard heights:\n\n```\npre_pos_z = 1.05   # ~7 cm above objects at z\u22480.97\ncarry_z   = 1.10   # safe traversal, well under upper limit 1.15\nrelease_z = 1.01   # ~7 cm above plate top at z\u22480.90\n```\n\nThese are *altitude* knobs (robot-frame), not object positions \u2014 you still derive\nthe object's **xy** by `back_project` every time.\n\n### 3.2. xy single-step \u00b10.30 cap\n\nOSC flips IK branches if you command `|x|>0.30` or `|y|>0.30` in a single\n`move_to` (the eef lands in the wrong half-space and corrupts the run). **Never\ncommand beyond \u00b10.30 in a single move** \u2014 split into carry-z waypoints. (Detail\nin `env_calibration.md`.)\n\n### 3.3. Slow long-distance carry for swap variants\n\nP2 swap can move a large object 15 cm across the table. `step_clip=0.025` lets it\nslip in the gripper and the object ends up centimetres off target. Mitigation\n(proven on `_swap` t0, carries over directly):\n\n- `carry_z = 1.15` (higher than usual 1.10)\n- `step_clip = 0.020` (slower)\n- **Re-localize mid-travel** instead of trusting a cached `object_xyz - eef_xyz`\n  offset: inspect `agentview_high.png` mid-carry and call `back_project` on the\n  carried object's pixel; if the\n  perceived offset drifted >5 mm from post-pick, re-pre-position and re-`pi0_pick`.\n\n### 3.4. Task language is from BDDL, not filename\n\nAfter the patch, `get_task(i).language` returns the perturbed `:language` tag, and\nthe env passes it to Pi0 as the prompt (surfaced as top-level `task_language`\nin each returned state view). For `_task` and `_lan` this is the perturbed\ninstruction. **Don't override it** \u2014 falsifying the VLA's prompt-blindness is the\npoint. You read the same language to decide *which* object to localize and place.\n\n### 3.5. \u26a0 Swap moves FIXTURES too \u2014 and you must localize them visually\n\nThis is the biggest perception-mode trap, and it is **specific to `_swap`**. The\nP2 perturbation does not only swap loose objects; for `libero_goal_swap` it swaps\nentire **fixtures** (stove \u2194 cabinet \u2194 wine_rack), so a goal predicate like\n`On(bowl, flat_stove_1_cook_region)` now points at wherever the *stove* was\nrelocated to, and there are no coordinates in the returned `state`. See\n`memory/libero/global/feedback_swap_perturbs_fixtures.md`.\n\nIn **oracle** mode the documented fix is to read the swap BDDL `:init` block and\nrecompute the fixture site's world coordinates. **That is forbidden here** \u2014 the\n`:init` block is ground-truth geometry. In **perception** mode you instead:\n\n1. Identify the target fixture by name from the (perturbed) task language and\n   `state.object_names`.\n2. **Localize the fixture's predicate site visually** in\n  `agentview_high.png`: pick pixels on the stove's cook region /\n   cabinet top surface / rack top shelf, then `back_project` (sample 3\u20135 pixels,\n   median the xy \u2014 fixtures are large and the surface you want is the *placement*\n   surface, not the nearest edge).\n3. Carry target xy = perceived site xy; descend to the perceived site z + a small\n   clearance, `release`, then **retreat with gripper open** \u2014 the predicate often\n   fires *during* the settle, not at the release step itself.\n\nSo the swap-fixture problem becomes \"find the fixture in the image\" rather than\n\"read where the BDDL put it.\" Loose-object swaps (e.g. `libero_spatial_swap`,\nwhere only bowls/plates move) are simpler: just localize each object by\n`back_project` as usual; their new positions fall straight out of the depth.\n\n### 3.6. Two cameras \u2014 agentview = IDENTITY, wrist = GEOMETRY\n\nDo **not** restate the two-camera protocol here \u2014 the strict guide's **First-step\nperception protocol** is the source of truth: `agentview.png` / `agentview_high.png` is the\nsemantic identity authority (decides *which* object/surface satisfies the task\nlanguage + relation); `wrist.png` / `wrist_high.png` refines geometry for the *same* candidate\n(accept only within ~3\u20135 cm of the agentview anchor, never average, basket/cavity\nexcepted). Both maps share one world frame, read via\n`back_project({\"camera\":\"wrist\"})`; the optional `segment` honours both cameras\n(`{\"camera\":\"agentview\"|\"wrist\"}`, `min_score` default 0.2, or `point:[row,col]`).\n\n**PRO implication:** `_swap` (and `_task`) can *invert* target or destination\nsemantics, so **never reuse base-task or recipe coordinates** \u2014 the object\nsatisfying the relation may now sit where the base task never put it. Let\nagentview decide *what*; the near-vertical wrist is BAD at identity (locks onto\nlook-alikes) and only refines *where*.\n\n### 3.6c. Mandatory pre-task perception pass\n\nAlso owned by the strict guide's First-step perception protocol: before ANY\npick/place, build the localization table (one row per task-relevant entity) and\npass the FINAL READY CHECK. **PRO implication:** in single-attempt mode a\nwrong-target first grab is unrecoverable, and under `_swap` the \"right\" target is\nexactly the one you'd get wrong by habit \u2014 so localizing all entities up front is\ncheap insurance, not optional.\n\n### 3.6b. Hi-res perception channel (1024\u00d71024)\n\nAlso owned by the strict guide's Hi-res perception channel section (1024\u00d71024\npairs each step; prefer the hi image for identification; `back_project` defaults\n`resolution=\"high\"`; never mix pixel grids). **PRO implication:** hi-res fixes\n*which* object you point at \u2014 decisive for the recolored/rescaled Object axis and\nfor telling swapped same-shape groceries apart \u2014 but does NOT change metric\naccuracy or replace the wrist coarse\u2192fine refinement. **Identity caveat:** SAM3\ngives two same-shaped, different-brand objects the SAME category (it labelled the\ntomato-sauce and alphabet-soup cans identically) \u2014 use its mask only as a category\ncandidate, then READ the label yourself in the hi-res crop to assign identity;\nnever let SAM3's category settle a brand choice.\n\n## 4. The four-cell experiment per (base task, seed)\n\nFor each base task you claim coverage on, generate four runs \u2014 every run is a\nperception cell:\n\n| Suite | Variant | Perception-mode expectation |\n|---|---|---|\n| `libero_spatial`        | base sanity | Pi0 and hybrid both pass |\n| `libero_spatial_task`   | **P1 Task** | Pi0 \u2717 (picks base target); hybrid \u2713 (LLM flips target from instruction, then localizes it) |\n| `libero_spatial_swap`   | **P2 Position** | Pi0 mixed; hybrid \u2713 \u2014 **by localizing the swapped object/fixture from depth, not from state** |\n| `libero_spatial_lan`    | Semantic | Both pass (paraphrase invariant) |\n\nReplace `spatial` with `object`, `goal`, or `10` for the other base suites.\n\n### 4.1. Hybrid run \u2014 the MCP runner\n\nLaunch a cell with the CLI; the runner owns `env_server.py`, exposes the\nstructured tools, and runs single-attempt:\n\n```bash\npython rpent/cli/main.py --robot libero --suite <suite> --task <n> --seed <k> \\\n    --libero-type pro --planner claude_code --model claude-opus-4-8\n\n# e.g. --suite libero_spatial_task 0 (P1) \u00b7 libero_spatial_swap 0 (P2) \u00b7\n#      libero_goal_swap 2 (P2 fixture swap) \u00b7 libero_10_task 5 (long horizon)\n```\n\n`--libero-type pro` may be given as `LIBERO_TYPE=pro` instead.\n\nAudit + recipe land in the run's `output_dir` (`output_dir` and `recipe_tag`\narrive in your first message):\n\n```\n{output_dir}/{recipe_tag}.json           <- you write this (write_text_file)\n{output_dir}/{recipe_tag}_recipe.jsonl   <- exported automatically by the runner\n```\n\nDo NOT write into `memory/libero/task-specific/` \u2014 that tree is a **read-only\nseed-0 reference corpus**, not a write target.\n\n### 4.2. Environment server is runner-owned\n\nThere is no manual server to launch and no REPL to drive: the MCP runner starts,\nmanages, and tears down `env_server.py` for you and blocks each tool call until\nthe next state record is dumped. Do not start, stop, or background it, and\ndo not poll for readiness.\n\n### 4.3. Pi0 fullshot baseline\n\nThe baseline is Pi0.5 driving the task end-to-end with the runtime's own\n(perturbed) `task_language`. There is **no standalone baseline CLI in this repo**;\nthe numbers to compare against are the recorded full-shot results (the team's\n`SUCCESS_RATES` table). **Do not invent a `pi0_baseline.py` path.**\n\nPi0 never sees object coords in either mode, so there is no perception variant of\nthe baseline. Expected behavior:\n\n- **P1 (task):** Pi0 \"succeeds at the wrong task\" \u2014 it picks the *base*-task\n  target object, places it on the plate, and `terminated=False` because the\n  goal predicate names a different object. This is exactly the gap the hybrid\n  closes.\n- **P2 (position):** Pi0 picks / places at the *base* (un-swapped) location.\n\n### 4.4. Audit JSON \u2014 PRO + perception fields\n\nStart from the `strict_perception` audit schema in the perception protocol, and\nadd the PRO fields:\n\n```jsonc\n{\n  \"suite\": \"libero_spatial_swap\",\n  \"task_id\": 0,\n  \"seed\": 0,\n  \"regime\": \"strict_perception\",\n  \"perturbation_type\": \"swap (P2 Position perturbation)\",\n  \"perturbed_task_language\": \"<actual task_language returned by view_env_state(step=0)>\",\n  \"perturbation_semantics\": \"<what swapped \u2014 objects and/or fixtures>\",\n  \"expected_baseline_behavior\": \"<predicted Pi0 failure: e.g. picks base location>\",\n  \"strategy_notes\": \"HOW you localized \u2014 which pixel(s) in agentview_high.png, back-projected world xyz; for swap, how you found the relocated object/fixture\",\n  \"pick_result\": { /* the pi0_pick step's result */ },\n  \"final_state\": { /* latest view_env_state result's `state` field */ },\n  \"terminated\": true\n}\n```\n\n`strategy_notes` **must** describe the localization (pixel \u2192 `back_project` \u2192\nworld xyz). For swap cells, explicitly note that the relocated object/fixture was\nfound by perception, not by reading coords. If unrecoverable after honest\nexploration, write `terminated: false` with what you tried and which step\nfailed \u2014 never warp (teleport primitives are deleted; see Rule 4 in the\nperception protocol). Write the audit with `write_text_file` to\n`{output_dir}/{recipe_tag}.json`, then call `finish`.\n\n## 5. Perception-protocol rules \u2014 PRO clarifications\n\n- **Rule 0 (use images for reasoning).** Even more critical under PRO: P2 swap can\n  move a large object/fixture clear across the table, and you have *no*\n  coordinates to fall back on. `agentview_high.png` plus `back_project` are your\n  spatial truth \u2014 inspect the embedded image and describe the scene before deciding\n  targets.\n- **Rule 1 (no `pi0_end_to_end`).** Pi0 does the grasp via `pi0_pick`; the LLM\n  scripts every motion + release. Under PRO this is doubly important \u2014 handing\n  back to Pi0 means handing back to the prompt-blind / memorized-place habit you\n  are trying to falsify.\n- **Rule 2 (single-episode current run).** This is a one-shot eval: do not call\n  `reset` or `exit`. Recover *within* the episode when safe (re-localize,\n  re-pre-position, re-`pi0_pick`, walk the prompt ladder,\n  `rotate_pitch`/`move_pose`); otherwise write an honest stuck/failure audit and\n  `finish`. `_swap` typically needs an in-episode retry \u2014 document it.\n- **Rule 4 (no teleport).** `set_object_pose`, `articulate_to`, `js_move_to`,\n  `carry_object` are deleted. A goal past OSC reach with no physical approach \u2192\n  honest `terminated:false`.\n- **Rule 5 (assume solvable).** A localization that moves the gripper into thin\n  air means you picked a wrong pixel (a reflection, a rim, a decoy object under\n  the Object perturbation), not that the cell is unreachable. Re-look, re-pick,\n  re-`back_project` before concluding failure.\n\n## 6. Existing corpus\n\n```\nrobots/libero/guides/\n\u251c\u2500\u2500 pro_hybrid_guide.md                 <- this file\n\u251c\u2500\u2500 strict_hybrid_guide.md              <- perception protocol + Rules (source of truth)\n\u2514\u2500\u2500 env_calibration.md                  <- OSC frame bounds + safe altitudes\nscripts/\n\u2514\u2500\u2500 liberopro_register_perturbations.patch\nmemory/libero/\n\u251c\u2500\u2500 MEMORY.md                  <- corpus index\n\u251c\u2500\u2500 global/                    <- feedback_* / project_* notes\n\u251c\u2500\u2500 task-family/                     <- task-level strategy, reusable across seeds\n\u2514\u2500\u2500 task-specific/                 <- seed-0 reference recipes, all suites\n```\n\nAfter inspecting the initial `task_language` and images, **read the auto-memory**:\n`memory/libero/MEMORY.md`. For perception PRO cells always\nopen `memory/libero/global/feedback_no_teleport_rule.md` and \u2014 for any `_swap` cell \u2014\n`memory/libero/global/feedback_swap_perturbs_fixtures.md` (what swaps, and why you re-find the\nrelocated fixture visually). For bowl\u2192plate spatial tasks also read\n`memory/libero/global/feedback_bowl_eef_y_offset.md`; for cluttered picks, `memory/libero/global/feedback_pi0_pick_full_prompt.md`;\nafter two failed retries, `memory/libero/global/feedback_failure_forensics.md`. The\n`memory/libero/task-specific/` recipes are **inputs** (technique priors) \u2014\nconsult them for prompt ladders, staging, and target zones, but never reuse their\ncoordinates (re-derive every xyz from THIS scene) and never write there.\n\n## 7. What to do next (priority order)\n\n1. **Extend spatial to all 10 tasks at seed 0**, four perception cells each\n   (base / `_task` / `_swap` / `_lan`). For hybrid runs, use the seed-0 reference\n   recipes in `memory/libero/task-specific/` as *technique* starting\n   points \u2014 the pick step is usually identical; the place target changes for\n   `_swap`, the target object changes for `_task`. Never reuse their coordinates.\n2. **Scale to seeds beyond 0** (50 trials per task). Recipes must re-localize per\n   scene; a perception recipe is *data-flow* (perceive \u2192 plan \u2192 act), never\n   hard-coded xyz.\n3. **Replicate on `libero_object`, `libero_goal`, `libero_10`.** Frame split\n  applies (read the initial returned `state.robot0_eef_pos[2]`). For\n   `libero_goal_swap`, apply \u00a73.5 \u2014 localize the **swapped fixture** visually.\n4. **Aggregate into a main table** `(suite \u00d7 perturbation \u00d7 {Pi0, hybrid})`. The\n   headline number is the conditional: of the seeds Pi0 fails on, what fraction\n   does the *perception* hybrid solve? Because there is no oracle state anywhere in\n   the hybrid's reasoning here, this is the strongest single statistic the agentic\n   decomposition can claim.\n\n## 8. Quick reference for a brand-new session\n\n```bash\n# 1. Sanity-check the liberopro patch (perturbed language must show)\nLIBERO_TYPE=pro python -c \\\n  \"import liberopro.liberopro.benchmark as b; print(b.get_benchmark('libero_spatial_task')().get_task(0).language)\"\n# -> must read 'Pick the akita black bowl not between ...' (the perturbed text)\n\n# 2. Launch a perception cell (runner owns env_server; single-attempt)\npython rpent/cli/main.py --robot libero --suite libero_spatial_swap --task <N> --seed 0 \\\n    --libero-type pro --planner claude_code --model claude-opus-4-8\n```\n\nThen, inside the run:\n\n3. `view_env_state({\"step\": 0})` \u2192 read `task_language` and\n   `state.robot0_eef_pos[2]` to pick the frame (\u00a73.1). Inspect\n   `agentview_high.png` and call `view_camera_meta`.\n4. Read `memory/libero/MEMORY.md` and the memories and seed-0 references that\n   match the current task. Localize the target (and, for `_swap`, the relocated\n   object/fixture) with `back_project` \u2014 run the mandatory pre-task perception pass\n   (\u00a73.6c). Plan, then execute one structured tool at a time.\n5. `write_text_file` the audit to `{output_dir}/{recipe_tag}.json`\n   (`regime: strict_perception`) before `finish`; the recipe\n   `{output_dir}/{recipe_tag}_recipe.jsonl` is exported automatically by the runner.\n\nWhen in doubt about *how to localize* or a primitive, the source of truth is\n[`strict_hybrid_guide.md`](./strict_hybrid_guide.md); about *PRO setup /\nperturbation semantics*, see\n[`scripts/install_libero_pro_plus.sh`](../../../../scripts/install_libero_pro_plus.sh)\nand \u00a72.\n"

What's different from oracle PRO mode (read this first)\\n\\n| | oracle mode (not in this repo) | **perception (this guide)** |\\n|---|---|---|\\n| how launched | oracle-state run | `rpent/cli/main.py --libero-type pro` (or `LIBERO_TYPE=pro`); perception artifacts always dumped, coords withheld |\\n| returned `state` objects | full `objects:{name:[x,y,z]}` | **`object_names:[\\u2026]` only \\u2014 NO coords** |\\n| how you learn the task | env prompt / scrape BDDL | **initial `task_language` returned by `view_env_state`** (authoritative `:language`, coord-free) \\u2014 never read the BDDL |\\n| extra obs artifacts | agentview RGB only | **logical agentview/wrist image, depth, world-map, and metadata keys, including `agentview_high.png` and `wrist_high.png`** \\u2014 geometry consumed through `back_project` |\\n| cameras | agentview only | **agentview (fixed, ~1m \\u2192 \\u00b18-13cm) + eye-in-hand wrist (moves with gripper, \\u00b11-2cm when <20cm to target)** \\u2014 see \\u00a73.6 |\\n| how P2 swap is solved | read swapped coords from oracle state | **localize the swapped objects by `back_project`** |\\n| how a swapped *fixture* site is found | read the swap BDDL `:init` block | **localize the fixture visually** (see \\u00a73.5) |\\n| run budget | short | **larger** \\u2014 perception localization + manipulation is slower (raise `--max-turns` / `--planner-timeout-s`) |\\n| audit `regime` | `strict` | `strict_perception` |\\n| which image you pick pixels in | agentview RGB | `agentview_high.png` (or `agentview.png` at low resolution) for **pixel-picking**; never use `agentview_policy.png` for back-projection |\\n\\n**The single most important conceptual shift.** In oracle PRO mode the headline\\nis \\\"the hybrid beats Pi0 on P2 because it reads the *swapped* coordinates straight\\nout of oracle state, while Pi0 is prompt-/memory-blind.\\\" In **perception**\\nmode there are no coordinates to read \\u2014 so the hybrid's P2 win now comes from\\n**seeing where the object is and back-projecting it**. This is a *stronger* claim\\n(no oracle state at all), and it is the whole point of running PRO in perception\\nmode: P1 (Task) is still won by reading the *language*; P2 (Position) is now won by\\n*perception*, not by an oracle.\\n\\n## 1. Why LIBERO-Pro\\n\\nLIBERO-Pro\\n([paper](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.03827), [repo](https://github.com/Zxy-MLlab/LIBERO-PRO))\\nperturbs each base task along five axes; all end-to-end VLAs (OpenVLA / Pi0 /\\nPi0.5 / UniVLA) collapse on the two strongest:\\n\\n| Axis | Suffix | Paper column | What changes | Headline result |\\n|---|---|---|---|---|\\n| **Task** | `_task` | **P1** | Instruction + goal predicate inverted | All VLAs \\u2248 0.0 |\\n| **Position** | `_swap` | **P2** | Object **and fixture** initial positions swapped | All VLAs 0.0\\u20130.4 |\\n| Semantic | `_lan` | \\u2014 | Instruction paraphrased; goal unchanged | VLAs handle (memorize visual) |\\n| Object | `_object` | \\u2014 | Object appearance / colour / scale | VLA visual policy stressed |\\n| Environment | `_environment` | \\u2014 | Table / scene swapped | Visual policy stressed |\\n\\nThe agentic hybrid wins on **P1 and P2** by routing the language channel and the\\n*perceived* spatial-state channel through the LLM. Object and Environment\\nperturbations enter through the Pi0 vision channel and the hybrid inherits the\\nVLA's weakness there \\u2014 declare that upfront, don't oversell. **In perception\\nmode, the Object/Environment axes also stress your own localization** (a\\nrecolored or rescaled object is harder to pixel-pick), so lean on the\\nmulti-pixel-median tip from the perception protocol.\\n\\n## 2. Setup (do these once on a fresh checkout)\\n\\nRun the idempotent installer from the repo root:\\n\\n```bash\\nbash scripts/install_libero_pro_plus.sh\\n```\\n\\nIt does all four steps below: the liberopro editable install, applies the\\nbenchmark-registration patch, syncs the authoritative HF dataset snapshot, and\\nverifies with the `get_benchmark(...).get_task(0).language` check. The perception\\nobservables (depth + both cameras + hi-res) are unconditional once the runner\\nlaunches with `--libero-type pro`; there is nothing perception-specific to\\ninstall beyond the PRO setup itself.\\n\\n### 2.1. LIBERO-PRO repo\\n\\nCloned at `${LIBERO_PRO_PATH:-/path/to/LIBERO-PRO}/` from\\n`https://github.com/RLinf/LIBERO-PRO.git` and installed editable into the openpi\\nvenv:\\n\\n```bash\\npython -m pip show liberopro\\n# Name: liberopro  Version: 0.1.0  Location: ${LIBERO_PRO_PATH:-/path/to/LIBERO-PRO}\\n```\\n\\n### 2.2. Apply the benchmark-registration patch\\n\\nThe upstream `__init__.py` does **not** expose the 16 perturbation suites through\\n`get_benchmark()`. Our patch\\n[`scripts/liberopro_register_perturbations.patch`](../../../../scripts/liberopro_register_perturbations.patch)\\nadds them and overrides `Task.language` to read each BDDL's actual `:language`\\ntag (so the perturbed instruction reaches Pi0 / hybrid).\\n\\n```bash\\ncd ${LIBERO_PRO_PATH:-/path/to/LIBERO-PRO}\\ngit apply <repo-root>/scripts/liberopro_register_perturbations.patch\\n```\\n\\nIf already applied (likely), `git status -s` shows clean. If you reinstall\\nliberopro, re-apply.\\n\\n### 2.3. Huggingface dataset (authoritative)\\n\\nThe LIBERO-PRO git repo ships **incomplete / broken** init files for several\\nperturbation suites (e.g. `libero_spatial_swap` has 0 BDDLs; some\\n`libero_spatial_task` `.pruned_init` files are 0 bytes). Treat the git repo as\\nunreliable for perturbation data. The full, correct set lives on Huggingface\\n([`zhouxueyang/LIBERO-Pro`](https://huggingface.co/datasets/zhouxueyang/LIBERO-Pro)),\\npersisted locally at:\\n\\n```\\n${LIBEROPRO_DATASET_PATH:-/path/to/liberopro_hf}/\\n\\u251c\\u2500\\u2500 bddl_files/                    16 perturbation suites, 10 BDDLs each\\n\\u2514\\u2500\\u2500 init_files/                    16 perturbation suites, 10 init files each\\n```\\n\\nCovers `{libero_spatial, libero_object, libero_goal, libero_10} \\u00d7 {swap, task,\\nlan, object}`. The installer syncs this into the liberopro install (overwriting\\nthe broken upstream files); if the persistent copy is gone, re-download with:\\n\\n```bash\\npython -c \\\"\\nfrom huggingface_hub import snapshot_download\\nsnapshot_download(repo_id='zhouxueyang/LIBERO-Pro', repo_type='dataset',\\n                  local_dir='${LIBEROPRO_DATASET_PATH:-/path/to/liberopro_hf}',\\n                  allow_patterns=['bddl_files/**','init_files/**'])\\\"\\n```\\n\\n### 2.4. Verify\\n\\n```bash\\nLIBERO_TYPE=pro python -c \\\"\\nimport liberopro.liberopro.benchmark as bench\\nfor n in ['libero_spatial_task','libero_spatial_swap','libero_spatial_lan']:\\n    b = bench.get_benchmark(n)(); t = b.get_task(0)\\n    print(f'{n} t0: {t.language!r}  trials={len(b.get_task_init_states(0))}')\\\"\\n```\\n\\nExpected:\\n```\\nlibero_spatial_task t0: 'Pick the akita black bowl not between the plate and the ramekin and place it on the plate'  trials=50\\nlibero_spatial_swap t0: 'Pick the akita black bowl between the plate and the ramekin and place it on the plate'  trials=50\\nlibero_spatial_lan  t0: 'lift the black bowl between the plate and ramekin and set it on the plate'  trials=50\\n```\\n\\n## 3. PRO-specific environment gotchas\\n\\nEverything in `strict_hybrid_guide.md` applies. The following are **additional**\\nPRO constraints (most live in detail at [`env_calibration.md`](./env_calibration.md)).\\n\\n### 3.1. Three scene frames, picked per-task\\n\\nPRO scenes use one of three table fixtures; the eef home z differs by up to\\n~0.9 m.\\n\\n| Fixture | eef home z | Table top z | xy reachable | Where |\\n|---|---|---|---|---|\\n| `living_room_table` | \\u2248 0.68 | \\u2248 0.43 | `(x\\u2208\\u00b10.30, y\\u2208\\u00b10.30)` | basket / plate / pudding |\\n| `kitchen_table` | \\u2248 1.17 | \\u2248 0.90 | `(x\\u2208\\u00b10.30, y\\u2208\\u00b10.30)` | stove / cabinet / drawer / microwave |\\n| `object` (low table) | \\u2248 0.26 | \\u2248 0.0 | `(x\\u2208\\u00b10.30, y\\u2208\\u00b10.30)` | `libero_object` grocery-into-basket |\\n\\n**Mandatory check at session start: read `state.robot0_eef_pos[2]` from\\n`view_env_state({\\\"step\\\": 0})`.** \\u2248 0.68 \\u2192 LIVING_ROOM; \\u2248 1.17 \\u2192 KITCHEN;\\n\\u2248 0.26 \\u2192 OBJECT. Pick `pre_pos_z` / `carry_z` / `release_z` accordingly (per-item\\nOBJECT-frame altitudes are in\\n`memory/libero/global/project_libero_object_pro_done.md`). Sending a\\nwrong-frame z (e.g. KITCHEN coordinates while the env is in LIVING_ROOM frame)\\ncrashes the env worker (EOFError, silent state loss).\\n\\n> **Perception note.** This proprioceptive z is *not* an object coordinate \\u2014 it's\\n> the robot's own pose, which the returned `state` still gives you in perception mode.\\n> Reading it to pick the frame is fine. It also doubles as a free depth sanity\\n> check: your back-projected table z should sit \\u22480.25 m below eef home z (\\u22480.43 in\\n> LIVING_ROOM, \\u22480.90 in KITCHEN). If a back-projection lands far from that, you\\n> picked a wrong pixel.\\n\\nFor `libero_spatial_*` you are always in **KITCHEN frame**. Standard heights:\\n\\n```\\npre_pos_z = 1.05   # ~7 cm above objects at z\\u22480.97\\ncarry_z   = 1.10   # safe traversal, well under upper limit 1.15\\nrelease_z = 1.01   # ~7 cm above plate top at z\\u22480.90\\n```\\n\\nThese are *altitude* knobs (robot-frame), not object positions \\u2014 you still derive\\nthe object's **xy** by `back_project` every time.\\n\\n### 3.2. xy single-step \\u00b10.30 cap\\n\\nOSC flips IK branches if you command `|x|>0.30` or `|y|>0.30` in a single\\n`move_to` (the eef lands in the wrong half-space and corrupts the run). **Never\\ncommand beyond \\u00b10.30 in a single move** \\u2014 split into carry-z waypoints. (Detail\\nin `env_calibration.md`.)\\n\\n### 3.3. Slow long-distance carry for swap variants\\n\\nP2 swap can move a large object 15 cm across the table. `step_clip=0.025` lets it\\nslip in the gripper and the object ends up centimetres off target. Mitigation\\n(proven on `_swap` t0, carries over directly):\\n\\n- `carry_z = 1.15` (higher than usual 1.10)\\n- `step_clip = 0.020` (slower)\\n- **Re-localize mid-travel** instead of trusting a cached `object_xyz - eef_xyz`\\n  offset: inspect `agentview_high.png` mid-carry and call `back_project` on the\\n  carried object's pixel; if the\\n  perceived offset drifted >5 mm from post-pick, re-pre-position and re-`pi0_pick`.\\n\\n### 3.4. Task language is from BDDL, not filename\\n\\nAfter the patch, `get_task(i).language` returns the perturbed `:language` tag, and\\nthe env passes it to Pi0 as the prompt (surfaced as top-level `task_language`\\nin each returned state view). For `_task` and `_lan` this is the perturbed\\ninstruction. **Don't override it** \\u2014 falsifying the VLA's prompt-blindness is the\\npoint. You read the same language to decide *which* object to localize and place.\\n\\n### 3.5. \\u26a0 Swap moves FIXTURES too \\u2014 and you must localize them visually\\n\\nThis is the biggest perception-mode trap, and it is **specific to `_swap`**. The\\nP2 perturbation does not only swap loose objects; for `libero_goal_swap` it swaps\\nentire **fixtures** (stove \\u2194 cabinet \\u2194 wine_rack), so a goal predicate like\\n`On(bowl, flat_stove_1_cook_region)` now points at wherever the *stove* was\\nrelocated to, and there are no coordinates in the returned `state`. See\\n`memory/libero/global/feedback_swap_perturbs_fixtures.md`.\\n\\nIn **oracle** mode the documented fix is to read the swap BDDL `:init` block and\\nrecompute the fixture site's world coordinates. **That is forbidden here** \\u2014 the\\n`:init` block is ground-truth geometry. In **perception** mode you instead:\\n\\n1. Identify the target fixture by name from the (perturbed) task language and\\n   `state.object_names`.\\n2. **Localize the fixture's predicate site visually** in\\n  `agentview_high.png`: pick pixels on the stove's cook region /\\n   cabinet top surface / rack top shelf, then `back_project` (sample 3\\u20135 pixels,\\n   median the xy \\u2014 fixtures are large and the surface you want is the *placement*\\n   surface, not the nearest edge).\\n3. Carry target xy = perceived site xy; descend to the perceived site z + a small\\n   clearance, `release`, then **retreat with gripper open** \\u2014 the predicate often\\n   fires *during* the settle, not at the release step itself.\\n\\nSo the swap-fixture problem becomes \\\"find the fixture in the image\\\" rather than\\n\\\"read where the BDDL put it.\\\" Loose-object swaps (e.g. `libero_spatial_swap`,\\nwhere only bowls/plates move) are simpler: just localize each object by\\n`back_project` as usual; their new positions fall straight out of the depth.\\n\\n### 3.6. Two cameras \\u2014 agentview = IDENTITY, wrist = GEOMETRY\\n\\nDo **not** restate the two-camera protocol here \\u2014 the strict guide's **First-step\\nperception protocol** is the source of truth: `agentview.png` / `agentview_high.png` is the\\nsemantic identity authority (decides *which* object/surface satisfies the task\\nlanguage + relation); `wrist.png` / `wrist_high.png` refines geometry for the *same* candidate\\n(accept only within ~3\\u20135 cm of the agentview anchor, never average, basket/cavity\\nexcepted). Both maps share one world frame, read via\\n`back_project({\\\"camera\\\":\\\"wrist\\\"})`; the optional `segment` honours both cameras\\n(`{\\\"camera\\\":\\\"agentview\\\"|\\\"wrist\\\"}`, `min_score` default 0.2, or `point:[row,col]`).\\n\\n**PRO implication:** `_swap` (and `_task`) can *invert* target or destination\\nsemantics, so **never reuse base-task or recipe coordinates** \\u2014 the object\\nsatisfying the relation may now sit where the base task never put it. Let\\nagentview decide *what*; the near-vertical wrist is BAD at identity (locks onto\\nlook-alikes) and only refines *where*.\\n\\n### 3.6c. Mandatory pre-task perception pass\\n\\nAlso owned by the strict guide's First-step perception protocol: before ANY\\npick/place, build the localization table (one row per task-relevant entity) and\\npass the FINAL READY CHECK. **PRO implication:** in single-attempt mode a\\nwrong-target first grab is unrecoverable, and under `_swap` the \\\"right\\\" target is\\nexactly the one you'd get wrong by habit \\u2014 so localizing all entities up front is\\ncheap insurance, not optional.\\n\\n### 3.6b. Hi-res perception channel (1024\\u00d71024)\\n\\nAlso owned by the strict guide's Hi-res perception channel section (1024\\u00d71024\\npairs each step; prefer the hi image for identification; `back_project` defaults\\n`resolution=\\\"high\\\"`; never mix pixel grids). **PRO implication:** hi-res fixes\\n*which* object you point at \\u2014 decisive for the recolored/rescaled Object axis and\\nfor telling swapped same-shape groceries apart \\u2014 but does NOT change metric\\naccuracy or replace the wrist coarse\\u2192fine refinement. **Identity caveat:** SAM3\\ngives two same-shaped, different-brand objects the SAME category (it labelled the\\ntomato-sauce and alphabet-soup cans identically) \\u2014 use its mask only as a category\\ncandidate, then READ the label yourself in the hi-res crop to assign identity;\\nnever let SAM3's category settle a brand choice.\\n\\n## 4. The four-cell experiment per (base task, seed)\\n\\nFor each base task you claim coverage on, generate four runs \\u2014 every run is a\\nperception cell:\\n\\n| Suite | Variant | Perception-mode expectation |\\n|---|---|---|\\n| `libero_spatial`        | base sanity | Pi0 and hybrid both pass |\\n| `libero_spatial_task`   | **P1 Task** | Pi0 \\u2717 (picks base target); hybrid \\u2713 (LLM flips target from instruction, then localizes it) |\\n| `libero_spatial_swap`   | **P2 Position** | Pi0 mixed; hybrid \\u2713 \\u2014 **by localizing the swapped object/fixture from depth, not from state** |\\n| `libero_spatial_lan`    | Semantic | Both pass (paraphrase invariant) |\\n\\nReplace `spatial` with `object`, `goal`, or `10` for the other base suites.\\n\\n### 4.1. Hybrid run \\u2014 the MCP runner\\n\\nLaunch a cell with the CLI; the runner owns `env_server.py`, exposes the\\nstructured tools, and runs single-attempt:\\n\\n```bash\\npython rpent/cli/main.py --robot libero --suite <suite> --task <n> --seed <k> \\\\\\n    --libero-type pro --planner claude_code --model claude-opus-4-8\\n\\n# e.g. --suite libero_spatial_task 0 (P1) \\u00b7 libero_spatial_swap 0 (P2) \\u00b7\\n#      libero_goal_swap 2 (P2 fixture swap) \\u00b7 libero_10_task 5 (long horizon)\\n```\\n\\n`--libero-type pro` may be given as `LIBERO_TYPE=pro` instead.\\n\\nAudit + recipe land in the run's `output_dir` (`output_dir` and `recipe_tag`\\narrive in your first message):\\n\\n```\\n{output_dir}/{recipe_tag}.json           <- you write this (write_text_file)\\n{output_dir}/{recipe_tag}_recipe.jsonl   <- exported automatically by the runner\\n```\\n\\nDo NOT write into `memory/libero/task-specific/` \\u2014 that tree is a **read-only\\nseed-0 reference corpus**, not a write target.\\n\\n### 4.2. Environment server is runner-owned\\n\\nThere is no manual server to launch and no REPL to drive: the MCP runner starts,\\nmanages, and tears down `env_server.py` for you and blocks each tool call until\\nthe next state record is dumped. Do not start, stop, or background it, and\\ndo not poll for readiness.\\n\\n### 4.3. Pi0 fullshot baseline\\n\\nThe baseline is Pi0.5 driving the task end-to-end with the runtime's own\\n(perturbed) `task_language`. There is **no standalone baseline CLI in this repo**;\\nthe numbers to compare against are the recorded full-shot results (the team's\\n`SUCCESS_RATES` table). **Do not invent a `pi0_baseline.py` path.**\\n\\nPi0 never sees object coords in either mode, so there is no perception variant of\\nthe baseline. Expected behavior:\\n\\n- **P1 (task):** Pi0 \\\"succeeds at the wrong task\\\" \\u2014 it picks the *base*-task\\n  target object, places it on the plate, and `terminated=False` because the\\n  goal predicate names a different object. This is exactly the gap the hybrid\\n  closes.\\n- **P2 (position):** Pi0 picks / places at the *base* (un-swapped) location.\\n\\n### 4.4. Audit JSON \\u2014 PRO + perception fields\\n\\nStart from the `strict_perception` audit schema in the perception protocol, and\\nadd the PRO fields:\\n\\n```jsonc\\n{\\n  \\\"suite\\\": \\\"libero_spatial_swap\\\",\\n  \\\"task_id\\\": 0,\\n  \\\"seed\\\": 0,\\n  \\\"regime\\\": \\\"strict_perception\\\",\\n  \\\"perturbation_type\\\": \\\"swap (P2 Position perturbation)\\\",\\n  \\\"perturbed_task_language\\\": \\\"<actual task_language returned by view_env_state(step=0)>\\\",\\n  \\\"perturbation_semantics\\\": \\\"<what swapped \\u2014 objects and/or fixtures>\\\",\\n  \\\"expected_baseline_behavior\\\": \\\"<predicted Pi0 failure: e.g. picks base location>\\\",\\n  \\\"strategy_notes\\\": \\\"HOW you localized \\u2014 which pixel(s) in agentview_high.png, back-projected world xyz; for swap, how you found the relocated object/fixture\\\",\\n  \\\"pick_result\\\": { /* the pi0_pick step's result */ },\\n  \\\"final_state\\\": { /* latest view_env_state result's `state` field */ },\\n  \\\"terminated\\\": true\\n}\\n```\\n\\n`strategy_notes` **must** describe the localization (pixel \\u2192 `back_project` \\u2192\\nworld xyz). For swap cells, explicitly note that the relocated object/fixture was\\nfound by perception, not by reading coords. If unrecoverable after honest\\nexploration, write `terminated: false` with what you tried and which step\\nfailed \\u2014 never warp (teleport primitives are deleted; see Rule 4 in the\\nperception protocol). Write the audit with `write_text_file` to\\n`{output_dir}/{recipe_tag}.json`, then call `finish`.\\n\\n## 5. Perception-protocol rules \\u2014 PRO clarifications\\n\\n- **Rule 0 (use images for reasoning).** Even more critical under PRO: P2 swap can\\n  move a large object/fixture clear across the table, and you have *no*\\n  coordinates to fall back on. `agentview_high.png` plus `back_project` are your\\n  spatial truth \\u2014 inspect the embedded image and describe the scene before deciding\\n  targets.\\n- **Rule 1 (no `pi0_end_to_end`).** Pi0 does the grasp via `pi0_pick`; the LLM\\n  scripts every motion + release. Under PRO this is doubly important \\u2014 handing\\n  back to Pi0 means handing back to the prompt-blind / memorized-place habit you\\n  are trying to falsify.\\n- **Rule 2 (single-episode current run).** This is a one-shot eval: do not call\\n  `reset` or `exit`. Recover *within* the episode when safe (re-localize,\\n  re-pre-position, re-`pi0_pick`, walk the prompt ladder,\\n  `rotate_pitch`/`move_pose`); otherwise write an honest stuck/failure audit and\\n  `finish`. `_swap` typically needs an in-episode retry \\u2014 document it.\\n- **Rule 4 (no teleport).** `set_object_pose`, `articulate_to`, `js_move_to`,\\n  `carry_object` are deleted. A goal past OSC reach with no physical approach \\u2192\\n  honest `terminated:false`.\\n- **Rule 5 (assume solvable).** A localization that moves the gripper into thin\\n  air means you picked a wrong pixel (a reflection, a rim, a decoy object under\\n  the Object perturbation), not that the cell is unreachable. Re-look, re-pick,\\n  re-`back_project` before concluding failure.\\n\\n## 6. Existing corpus\\n\\n```\\nrobots/libero/guides/\\n\\u251c\\u2500\\u2500 pro_hybrid_guide.md                 <- this file\\n\\u251c\\u2500\\u2500 strict_hybrid_guide.md              <- perception protocol + Rules (source of truth)\\n\\u2514\\u2500\\u2500 env_calibration.md                  <- OSC frame bounds + safe altitudes\\nscripts/\\n\\u2514\\u2500\\u2500 liberopro_register_perturbations.patch\\nmemory/libero/\\n\\u251c\\u2500\\u2500 MEMORY.md                  <- corpus index\\n\\u251c\\u2500\\u2500 global/                    <- feedback_* / project_* notes\\n\\u251c\\u2500\\u2500 task-family/                     <- task-level strategy, reusable across seeds\\n\\u2514\\u2500\\u2500 task-specific/                 <- seed-0 reference recipes, all suites\\n```\\n\\nAfter inspecting the initial `task_language` and images, **read the auto-memory**:\\n`memory/libero/MEMORY.md`. For perception PRO cells always\\nopen `memory/libero/global/feedback_no_teleport_rule.md` and \\u2014 for any `_swap` cell \\u2014\\n`memory/libero/global/feedback_swap_perturbs_fixtures.md` (what swaps, and why you re-find the\\nrelocated fixture visually). For bowl\\u2192plate spatial tasks also read\\n`memory/libero/global/feedback_bowl_eef_y_offset.md`; for cluttered picks, `memory/libero/global/feedback_pi0_pick_full_prompt.md`;\\nafter two failed retries, `memory/libero/global/feedback_failure_forensics.md`. The\\n`memory/libero/task-specific/` recipes are **inputs** (technique priors) \\u2014\\nconsult them for prompt ladders, staging, and target zones, but never reuse their\\ncoordinates (re-derive every xyz from THIS scene) and never write there.\\n\\n## 7. What to do next (priority order)\\n\\n1. **Extend spatial to all 10 tasks at seed 0**, four perception cells each\\n   (base / `_task` / `_swap` / `_lan`). For hybrid runs, use the seed-0 reference\\n   recipes in `memory/libero/task-specific/` as *technique* starting\\n   points \\u2014 the pick step is usually identical; the place target changes for\\n   `_swap`, the target object changes for `_task`. Never reuse their coordinates.\\n2. **Scale to seeds beyond 0** (50 trials per task). Recipes must re-localize per\\n   scene; a perception recipe is *data-flow* (perceive \\u2192 plan \\u2192 act), never\\n   hard-coded xyz.\\n3. **Replicate on `libero_object`, `libero_goal`, `libero_10`.** Frame split\\n  applies (read the initial returned `state.robot0_eef_pos[2]`). For\\n   `libero_goal_swap`, apply \\u00a73.5 \\u2014 localize the **swapped fixture** visually.\\n4. **Aggregate into a main table** `(suite \\u00d7 perturbation \\u00d7 {Pi0, hybrid})`. The\\n   headline number is the conditional: of the seeds Pi0 fails on, what fraction\\n   does the *perception* hybrid solve? Because there is no oracle state anywhere in\\n   the hybrid's reasoning here, this is the strongest single statistic the agentic\\n   decomposition can claim.\\n\\n## 8. Quick reference for a brand-new session\\n\\n```bash\\n# 1. Sanity-check the liberopro patch (perturbed language must show)\\nLIBERO_TYPE=pro python -c \\\\\\n  \\\"import liberopro.liberopro.benchmark as b; print(b.get_benchmark('libero_spatial_task')().get_task(0).language)\\\"\\n# -> must read 'Pick the akita black bowl not between ...' (the perturbed text)\\n\\n# 2. Launch a perception cell (runner owns env_server; single-attempt)\\npython rpent/cli/main.py --robot libero --suite libero_spatial_swap --task <N> --seed 0 \\\\\\n    --libero-type pro --planner claude_code --model claude-opus-4-8\\n```\\n\\nThen, inside the run:\\n\\n3. `view_env_state({\\\"step\\\": 0})` \\u2192 read `task_language` and\\n   `state.robot0_eef_pos[2]` to pick the frame (\\u00a73.1). Inspect\\n   `agentview_high.png` and call `view_camera_meta`.\\n4. Read `memory/libero/MEMORY.md` and the memories and seed-0 references that\\n   match the current task. Localize the target (and, for `_swap`, the relocated\\n   object/fixture) with `back_project` \\u2014 run the mandatory pre-task perception pass\\n   (\\u00a73.6c). Plan, then execute one structured tool at a time.\\n5. `write_text_file` the audit to `{output_dir}/{recipe_tag}.json`\\n   (`regime: strict_perception`) before `finish`; the recipe\\n   `{output_dir}/{recipe_tag}_recipe.jsonl` is exported automatically by the runner.\\n\\nWhen in doubt about *how to localize* or a primitive, the source of truth is\\n[`strict_hybrid_guide.md`](./strict_hybrid_guide.md); about *PRO setup /\\nperturbation semantics*, see\\n[`scripts/install_libero_pro_plus.sh`](../../../../scripts/install_libero_pro_plus.sh)\\nand \\u00a72.\\n\"\n}"

Wrist samples: bowl interior floor (-0.2534,-0.16,0.9365); upper-left interior (-0.2223,-0.1194,0.9648); lower-right interior (-0.2756,-0.1945,0.9678).

Hmm the mapping: wrist image +col → ? +row → ? From these: pixel (560,570) → (-0.2534,-0.160); (470,450) → (-0.2223,-0.1194); (640,690)→(-0.2756,-0.1945).

So increasing row → more negative x and more negative y; increasing col → more negative y and less negative x... Let's linearize: 
Δ(row, col) from (560,570) to (640,690): Δrow=+80, Δcol=+120 → Δx=-0.0222, Δy=-0.0345.
From (560,570) to (470,450): Δrow=-90, Δcol=-120 → Δx=+0.0311, Δy=+0.0406.

Solve: a*Δrow + b*Δcol = Δx and c*Δrow + d*Δcol = Δy.
Eq1: 80a+120b = -0.0222
Eq2: -90a-120b = 0.0311 → 90a+120b = -0.0311
Subtract: (90-80)a = -0.0311+0.0222 = -0.0089 → a = -0.00089 per row.
Then 120b = -0.0222 - 80(-0.00089) = -0.0222+0.0712=0.049 → b=0.000408 per col.
For y: 80c+120d = -0.0345; 90c+120d = -0.0406 → 10c = -0.0061 → c=-0.00061; 120d = -0.0345-80(-0.00061)= -0.0345+0.0488=0.0143 → d=0.000119 per col.

Wrist plate center = (0.0664, 0.1940) — vs agentview plate center (0.0433, 0.2015). Δx = 2.3cm, Δy = 0.8cm. Within tolerance; wrist is more accurate at close range. Take plate center ≈ (0.058, 0.198) (midpoint leaning wrist) or just wrist (0.066,0.194). Hmm, 2.3cm difference. Note the wrist pixel-picked center is approximate since the ellipse spans 400-745 → col center 572, row 370-680 → row center 525. That's what I used. Good.

Hmm, but wait: is the visible plate ellipse's projected center the true center? Yes for a circle viewed obliquely, ellipse center ≈ projected center (approximately).

Bowl sample agentview (550,800) → (0.0161, 0.1974, 1.0576). That's a point on the bowl top surface. The bowl's center: bowl spans cols 710-890 → center col 800 (I picked 800), rows 495-600 → center row 547. So this sample is roughly the bowl's projected center: (0.016, 0.197).

So bowl center ≈ (0.016, 0.197); plate center ≈ (0.058, 0.198) (use wrist-influenced) or (0.043,0.201).

Required correction: bowl must move from x=0.016 to x≈0.05 → +0.034 in x. y is already fine (0.197 vs 0.198).

Hmm, interesting: y is aligned. So the offset correction: Δx = +0.035 (using plate x=0.058 → Δ=0.042). Let's use Δx = +0.038 → eef x = 0.0354+0.038 = 0.073, y stays ~0.2365.

Hmm, but careful: is the eef y=0.2365 too far? The bowl is at y=0.197 which matches the plate. Good, that offset was properly accounted.

But wait: the agentview bowl center sample z=1.0576 — that's the top rim of the bowl. Fine.

So new eef target: (0.073, 0.237, 1.08). Δx=0.038, small. Then descend to release height.

Release height: bowl bottom currently = rim_top(1.0576) - bowl_height. Bowl height: at grasp, rim was ~0.968 and bottom 0.926 → 4.2cm. So bowl bottom ≈ 1.0576-0.042 = 1.016. Hmm, that's pretty high above the plate (0.908). Need to descend ~0.108 to have the bowl bottom at 0.908.

Descend eef z by 0.108: from 1.0795 → 0.9715. So descend to eef z ≈ 0.972 (leaving a couple mm). Let's descend to 0.975 then fine-check, then to 0.965 with release.

Wait, better to descend in two steps and check with the wrist view at each. But careful: descending too low might drive the gripper fingers into the plate. The fingers at grasp were at eef z 0.9418 with the bowl bottom on the stove top 0.926, so fingers reach ~1.6cm below the bowl bottom... no: eef z 0.9418, bowl bottom 0.926 → the fingers extend 1.6cm below the eef center? Actually the eef position is the tool flange; fingers extend downward. At the grasp, eef z = 0.9418 and fingers reached down to ~0.926 or lower.

So if I descend to eef z = 0.972, the fingers bottom at 0.972-0.016 = 0.956, which is above the plate (0.908). Fine.

Then release: the bowl falls from bottom 1.016-0.108=0.908 → exactly on the plate. 

Hmm, so eef z=0.972 puts the bowl bottom right at the plate surface. Good.

Let's do: move_to (0.073, 0.237, 1.02) x&y first + partial descent, check wrist, then descend to 0.972.

Actually do 2 steps: (0.073, 0.237, 1.03) then (0.073, 0.237, 0.972), checking after the first with wrist to confirm alignment. Let's combine: first correct xy at z=1.08 (small move), inspect wrist, then descend.

Efficient: move_to (0.073, 0.237, 1.055) — corrects xy and lowers 2.5cm. Then check wrist. Then descend to 0.975, check, then release.

Hmm, each step costs time. Let's do move_to (0.073, 0.237, 1.00) directly (Δz=-0.08, small xy). Then inspect wrist for alignment, correct if needed, descend to 0.972, release.

Actually the memory warns: release high → topples. And also warns bowl may land off-center. Let's be careful and take the two-step descent.
